Objects outside the map (`_object_outside_of_map_bit`, as their outermost parent is: `OBJECT_OUTSIDE_OF_MAP`) are hidden now; but that room is in the same BSP as the beach (the game stayed in BSP 0), so they still showed. Objects in clusters the game's camera can't see from its own, by the BSP's potentially visible sets (`cluster_data`, `ClusterVisibility`), are hidden too, as the game leaves them undrawn: the cutscene's camera, in cluster 18, sees 4 of b30's 56 clusters, and every tree, shrub and boulder round the window is outside them. A camera flying free sees everything. Not yet seen in the cutscene (it couldn't be started by script).
   - [x] Indoor flicker worse since lights off screen stayed lit. Fixed (23 October) by the light tiles' seeding (third pass) and Solarik's light power weighting by its square root (`SOLARIK_FLUX_EXPONENT`, default 0.5): no lighting flicker reported in play since. The passes below are kept as a record of where to look if it returns. Leaving out fixtures and the game's lights further from the camera than their range and 10 m was tried and taken out (Guilty Spark's light went out as he flew off; on a still view of a10's corridor (level viewer, fixtures only) the frame-to-frame difference is the same with and without, 0.322 and 0.335). Seen in Forerunner interiors without alarm lights too, so the game's moving lights are not the cause either. Still to look at: the history `dynamic_scene` keeps through scene changes.
     - Second pass (19 October), in the level viewer with a still camera: frame-to-frame flicker is small (a10 under a ceiling strip 0.25, b30's dim room 0.07 mean change of 255); the boiling is a slow drift over hundreds of frames, which consecutive frames miss. Captured every 40 frames for 1600, b30's dim room varies by 12% of its brightness per pixel (strongest at the light pools, but over whole walls), b30's lit corridor by under 2%, and a10's ceiling swings fivefold (brightness 20 to 115 over a few hundred frames) though nothing in the view moves; Solarik's world cache view (`SOLARIK_SHADER_DEFS=VISUALIZE_WORLD_CACHE`) swings the same, so a10's drift is in the world cache. Not the cause (no change in either drift): a light grid for direct light candidates (ReGIR, Solarik branch `light-grid`, kept unmerged), a longer cache history, steadier cache change detection, history counted in cell updates alone, cells living 60 frames unqueried (those four as Solarik diagnostic definitions on that branch), fixture lights (`HALO_RT_NO_FIXTURE_LIGHTS=1`), animated glow (`HALO_RT_FREEZE_ANIMATION=1`), DLSS (still drifts without it; at the game's 1280x720 its Auto mode is already DLAA). Next: why a10's cache cells swing (GI rays between cells, the update budget's selection, hash collisions with nine BSPs' geometry), and a reference from Solarik's path tracer to tell drift from the lighting's true value.
     - Third pass (20 October): the slow drift was Solarik's light tiles. Bevy's generators use their seed as their state, and the tiles were seeded `tile * 5782582 + slot + frame`, so frame f + 1's slot i drew frame f's slot i + 1's light: the 131072 samples turned over in a thousand frames, and each workgroup (screen region, cache cells) read the same tile every frame. A light few samples land on (a10's ceiling strip, one of 596 emissive meshes beside a sun taking half the samples) went in and out of a region's candidates for hundreds of frames; the whole view, the panels beside the strip included, faded to near black (brightness 16) and back (114). Hashed seeds and a tile per workgroup per frame (Solarik b289514): drift a10 strip 0.74 to 0.33, a10 corridor 0.44 to 0.26, b30 room 0.12 to 0.10 (relative std over 40 captures); correlation between captures 40 frames apart 0.81 to 0.2-0.3, at 80 frames none; frame-to-frame change under the strip up (0.26 to 0.7-0.8 of 255: fresh samples every frame, which DLSS averages). After the fix, again no help (within run scatter): the light grid (rebased as `light-grid-2`; slightly noisier), the cache's longer history and longer cell life (the cache's own view still varies 40-60% a capture; 60-frame lives triple its cells to 310000, so most are touched only now and then by GI rays, and 7-10% are new each frame with a still camera), a 10% sun share (`SOLARIK_DIRECTIONAL_SHARE=0.1`: a10 strip 0.26, corridor unchanged). What is left is noise of tens of frames, the whole view wandering a few percent. `HALO_RT_CACHE_LOG=1` logs the cache's counters every 40 frames.
     - Fourth pass (21 October, from the user's capture series: b30's Shaft A passage and a10's corridor, patches of 50-100 pixels changing 15% (p90) to 30-37% (p99) in half a second, in shade only). Measured as patch-scale wander (each capture blurred over 19-pixel boxes, the per-pixel std over 40 captures 40 frames apart, relative), in the level viewer at the user's spots (scratchpad `boil2.sh`, `blotch.py`). Two causes, both in Solarik:
       - The world cache kept a few samples where 32 were meant. Its blend capped history at 16 frames' worth of updates (a10's 137000 cells, 40000 updated a frame: 4.7 samples) and cut it further by the cell's change through an eighth root, which single-sample noise always triggered. So each update replaced a large share of a cell with one light sample, one of hundreds of emitters, and the cache, which feeds itself, swung as a whole: a10's corridor faded between brightness 17 and 44 over a thousand frames, and the cache's own view changed 46% between captures. Now 128 frames' worth, the cut in proportion to the change (lights still switch within a few updates), 128 light candidates and 4 shadow-tested samples an update: the cache's view changes 4% between captures, a10's corridor swings 21-26.
       - ReSTIR DI reused a sample for up to 20 frames over 30 pixels, from 8 candidates of a workgroup's shared tile: patches of direct light that came and went, which DLSS-RR keeps as detail (b30's patches; ReSTIR GI's settings, the glossy path and the fixture lights made no difference). Now a confidence cap of 8 and 32 candidates; a smaller spatial radius was worse.
       - Wander, before and after: b30 passage 0.049 to 0.027 (the user's four-shot spread measure 0.185 to 0.105), a10 corridor 0.30 to 0.11, a10 second spot 0.25 to 0.056 (frame-to-frame 0.14 to 0.02); the beach unchanged (0.004). Some interiors come out a little brighter (a10's second spot 14.5 to 18.2): the noisy cache read darker. Cost 0.85 ms a frame on the RTX 5080 (world cache 0.3 to 0.8, direct 0.8 to 1.2). Left: a10's corridor still wanders about 10%, slowly; a 10% sun share helps there a little more (0.108 to 0.095) but was left alone for outdoors.
     - Fifth pass (21 October, the user: still prominent in the Pillar of Autumn's narrow corridors). The level viewer had misled: it draws all of a level's BSPs, and a10's nine overlap, so their walls shadowed each other's lights (a10's corridor 0.11 wander there, 0.012 with `--bsp 1` alone, as the game draws it). Still, the corridor is steady; the boiling is in motion, the denoiser's history short. Measured with the camera swaying along the corridor (`--dolly 1`, `HALO_RT_DOLLY_DURING=1`: captures at one phase of every sweep; scratchpad `boil_move.sh`) by the user's three-shot spread: 0.098 against the user's own 0.103.
       - Direct light made it (0.098 to 0.047 without it; the bounce and glossy paths made no difference). A diagnostic of each pixel's chosen light showed why: 74% of the corridor's pixels chose a light out of sight, mostly the sun, whose fixed half share of the light tiles, and unshadowed brightness, won the candidates indoors behind the hull. Solarik's ReSTIR DI resamples by unshadowed contribution and traces visibility only at the end; the reuse then spread the dark choice over 30 pixels and 8 frames.
       - Now the sun has a sample of its own in every pixel, shadow-tested before it competes (outdoors it reaches every pixel instead of half); the light tiles hold no sun samples (drawn again, the others' density corrected), and the world cache takes its own sun sample too; the chosen tile light is shadow-tested before reuse (as Solari did before the Bevy 0.20 move). In motion 0.098 to 0.061, b30's passage (still) 0.027 to 0.011, the beach unchanged; cost 0.4 ms (direct 1.1 to 1.5). More candidates (64, 128) made no difference.
       - Then the motion round the corridor's fixtures (not animated: `flourescent` is constant; the bridge's floor light, `steel light`, emits nothing). A shadow diagnostic found no housing over the tubes (no ray stopped at the light; 4% within 1 cm of the surface). Light candidates by place (Solarik, opt-in `SOLARIK_CELL_LIGHTS=1`: each world cache cell's four lights in sight, 8 candidates, balance heuristic with the tiles) 0.061 to 0.056 for 0.47 ms. What is left is spread: by region, near the fixtures, direct light off 0.022 against 0.026, glossy off 0.022, direct shadows off 0.026, bounce off worse (0.036); the floor of this approach, and DLSS in motion. Solarik's `GpuDirectionalLight` carries its light source index.
       - Then the Jefferies tubes (the user's worst case: a10's maintenance tubes, walls an arm's length away, lit by small recessed lights). The cell candidates went on by default (the user: it felt good). In the bridged game, with the player moved into the user's capture spot (OpenCE's new `HALO_SIM_TELEPORT`, as no flag marks it; a Grunt killed on the player for its plasma pistol, the tube's squad erased), the tube's walls were steady unarmed (0.03) and boiled holding the plasma pistol (0.23): the hidden first-person body's weapon glowed, and Solarik took every emissive instance for a light, this one at the camera among the hands holding it, its shadow rays coming and going. A masked body's emission is now no light (rays that meet it still see it glow): armed, 0.02-0.03. (In the level viewer the tubes never boiled: it has no player.)
       - The user still saw the tubes boil (22 October), with the plasma pistol and with the magnum, and less with the flashlight on. A logged playthrough (`HALO_RT_LIGHT_LOG`) found a10's alarm light (`levels\a10\devices\alert_light\alert_light`, red, two rotating beams) lit all through the tube, 1.3 world units from the camera: it hangs in BSP 3's room, which overlaps BSP 4's tube, in BSP 4's solid. The game's lights in the drawn BSP's solid (and 0.1 world units round them) are now left out (`LightsInSolid`); a10's distant burning wreckage flames go too. The user: "dramatically better", acceptable; some flicker remains beside the dim red strips (emitted power 10 against the fluorescents' 80, so rarely sampled).
       - The flicker beside the strips (asked to fix, 22 October): the wall beside a strip was lit and dark by turns, 50 frames at a time. Solarik's `VISUALIZE_CELL_LIGHTS` (new) showed half the screen finding no world cache cell, so no light candidates by place: a camera pixel keyed its cell with its normal-mapped normal, the cells were made with the GI hits' normals, and a wall's zero component (floor(2n): 0 or -1 by its sign) told them apart. The lookup now tries both sides of such a step (cells found 48% to 77%), and the cells' light slots are weighted by each light's shadow-tested irradiance there (ReSTIR DI and the cache's own search draw them by weight). The wall beside the strip: three-shot spread 0.35 to 0.01, brighter (the strip found every frame); the view's p90 0.053 to 0.022; a10's corridor in motion 0.056 to 0.049. +0.6 ms. Splitting an emitting BSP material into its fixtures (one light each) did no better, and was left out. Solarik's GPU tests `cache_history`, `cache_priority` and `restir_shadow` fail on identifiers their fixtures lack (`WORLD_CACHE_HISTORY_FRAMES`, `light_tile_seed`), from earlier commits.
     - Scripted captures (`--screenshot`) now open without taking the focus and without the free camera's controls (keys and the mouse moved the camera between captures, and the window took the user's keyboard), and `--dolly` counts the same frames as the captures and, with `--screenshot-every`, stops at `--screenshot-from`.
   - [x] A clear coat (a glossy layer over a coloured base). Solarik takes StandardMaterial's clearcoat, clearcoat_perceptual_roughness and (with its `pbr_multi_layer_material_textures` feature) clearcoat_texture: from the G-buffer for primary surfaces and its material data for rays; a coat lobe (Filament's: f0 4%, its own roughness) over the surface, which sees what the coat lets through; sampled as a third lobe, its own reflection ray in the specular pass, either glossy lobe on glossy bounces, and DLSS-RR's guides. The viewer's baked metallic-roughness textures carry the shader's reflection mask in red, as the coat's mask. Tried on the Elites' armour (an Elite placed on b30's beach, `HALO_RT_PLACE`, with a stand-in change colour, `HALO_RT_ELITE_COLOR`): over their near-mirror metal a coat hardly shows; over a duller base (`HALO_RT_ELITE_SHINE=0.4`) it gives the lacquered look, but the near-mirror metal as it is looked best, so the Elites have no coat (`HALO_RT_ELITE_COAT=1,0.1` gives them one).
   - [x] (Outdoors "fantastic, great image stability", 20 October, after the light tile seeds.) Still unstable outdoors: alpha-tested foliage (trees are the brightest areas in a flicker map of a still view), noise in the shade (lit by the sky and bounces alone), distant silhouettes.
   - [ ] An older halt in debug.txt (13 October, 18:39): `hud_nav_points.c #615: waypoint_type!=NONE`. Perhaps the input and sound loss seen then; not reproduced.
   - [x] (It follows looking up and down, 20 October.) The flashlight "bound to horizontal movement" (a10, b30): it is aimed along the game's camera, pitch included (`HALO_RT_LIGHT_LOG` shows it). Check in play whether it still doesn't follow looking up and down.
   - [ ] Once, sound and input stopped while the picture kept going (perhaps a misclicked shortcut). Watch whether the game process exits.
   - The user's play session of 22 October (a10, b30; the Silent Cartographer's and Halo's water "great"):
     - [x] The Jefferies tubes boil more again since the latest lighting changes. On 23 October the user found them about as when called playable.
     - [x] Inside the drop pod (a30's crashed lifepod, round the player at the start), now and then a flash of the outside (too brief to capture). Objects in clusters the game's camera can't see are hidden, and an object's cluster was its centre's alone; the game draws an object in every cluster its bounds touch. The pod is large and the start's camera passes through clusters 1, 33 and 34 (logged with the new `HALO_RT_HIDE_LOG=1`: each object shown or hidden, and why), so a camera crossing into a cluster that sees the pod's centre's not hid the pod round it. Now an object is hidden only when none of the clusters under its bounding sphere (centre, axes, the enclosing cube's corners: `BOUNDS_SAMPLES`) is seen, and never with the camera inside its sphere. The flash itself wasn't reproduced by script (it needs the player's movement); the start's hiding is otherwise unchanged.
       - 23 October: still flashing, with no input right after the cutscene, the pod's outside flickering and its inside boiling as the tubes once did. The pod round the player (`lifepod_crashed` 0xe3d30164) was made anew 2573 times in 5000 frames: its change colour follows a flickering function (its lights), its self-illumination animates, and an object with animated self-illumination whose stepped colours changed was made again rather than recoloured, its new entities now and then not drawn yet as the old retired. Now its animation takes the game's colours as they are every frame, and its pieces are recoloured in place (the animated ones' own material copies given the coloured base map). Made again 0 times; 20 captures 3 frames apart from frame 3600 steady. (`HALO_RT_HIDE_LOG=1` also logs each object made again, and why.)
     - [x] b30's sky: cloud layers come and go as the view pitches up and down (the user's beach series, holding the rifle). Aligned on the horizon, the series showed the ring drawn over the clouds whenever the view pitched down past a point. The sky's layers keep Halo's part order by a sort bias added to their bounds' centres' distances, in steps of 10⁶ m, but at the sky's scale the ring's centre is 1.6·10⁶ m up (the clouds' 5·10⁴): pitching down, it sorted after the clouds. Steps are now 10⁸ (`SKY_LAYER_BIAS`; the largest centre is ~2·10⁶ m out).
     - [x] The pistol's zoom shows vertical streaks at the horizon (the stretching reported before; perhaps stars reflected in the sea). Likely the fog below DLAA (next item's 23 October note): at Performance b30's far sea was a white band streaked vertically, gone with the fog's depth read in the viewport. The user plays at Auto.
     - [x] Below DLAA, a faint copy of the scene at render resolution in the screen's corner, and black outlines round everything against the sky and the sea (worse at lower qualities). Both were the sky's RR overlay: below DLAA the view is drawn in the render-resolution corner of full-size textures, and the overlay was full size; RR takes the overlay's resolution from its texture, so it composited the corner 1:1 over its output and its sky mask fell beside the edges. The overlay is now the render resolution's size (`MainPassResolutionOverride`). Checked on b30's shore at Performance, Ultra Performance and DLAA: no corner copy, no outlines, the stars kept.
       - 23 October: the modes fixed, but a slight outline round models against the sea's fog. The fog pass (after upscaling) read the depth over the whole depth texture, which below DLAA is the picture's size with the view drawn in its corner: each point's fog came from a depth elsewhere (at Performance, twice as far from the corner). It now reads it in the main pass's viewport, and filters the fog of the four render pixels round where the frame's jittered view saw the point (`halo_fog.wesl`). Frames alternating the two readings at Performance, and at DLAA, on b30's shore: the new at Performance matches both at DLAA; the old's far sea was a white, vertically streaked band.
     - [x] The needler's needles seem to mask the water's fog seen through them. They did: since 16 October first-person panes write their own depth, so the fog pass leaves the needles clear (the game draws the weapon after its fog), and so it fogged what they let through only as far as the needles. Solarik now fogs the background behind the camera's first pane within `SolarikNearRays`' reach (a first-person weapon's) with the ray fog, from the pane to the surface behind. Shaders validated; not yet seen in play (no scripted way to put a needler in the player's hands over the sea). A level pane within a metre of the camera would have its background fogged twice.
       - 23 October: the needles drawn behind the sky again where it is behind them. The panes' sky mask must draw before the sky's layers, but its sort bias (-10⁹) was above the sky's once that was -2·10⁹ (the cloud fix above), so the sky's first layers drew over them first. The mask's bias is now -2.1·10⁹ (sort order only: a material's bias is the GPU's depth bias for StandardMaterial alone). Not seen in play by script.
     - [x] Materials: many models looked rubbery (the Warthog, crewmen). Pass of 24 October against MCC references: dim model reflections' unmasked parts fade toward matte (crew cloth); the Warthog and Pelican are paint, not metal (`MODEL_METAL`); Chief's black suit is cloth; the Elites' and Chief's armour plates carry Solarik's thin-film clear coat (320 nm), the iridescence of Halo's painted cube maps. Found on the way: Bevy's deferred prepass ignored clear coat textures (fixed in Solarik's vendored bevy_pbr).
     - [ ] Performance seems a little lower with this batch of changes. Not found by measurement (23 October): `HALO_RT_GPU_LOG=1` logs each render pass's GPU time every 5 s (spans not timed in the last second left out: a span timed only on the frames it runs kept its average from the load's BLAS builds), and `HALO_RT_NO_VSYNC=1` presents unsynchronized. In the bridged game at the user's Jefferies tube spot, this build against the morning's (bevy-rt-test 137943a, Solarik 8776af3) in worktrees: Solarik's passes 7.30 against 7.59 ms, the same frame rate (scripted windows are held near 60 even unsynchronized). Today's features each cost nothing measurable on b30 (sky overlay, ripples: the glass pass is 0.17 ms either way; cell lights). The largest cost found is old: in the bridged game, BLAS builds take 2.2 ms of every frame (b30's beach, 10.9 ms in all), the skinned characters' meshes copied each frame and built afresh. Refitting their BLAS in place instead would save most of it. Still to know: where the user felt it.
       - 23 October, from the user's screenshots (a10 and b30 wherever the game's frame rate read 70 or below, at DLAA): the GPU is the limit; the viewer's CPU takes 1.8 ms on the main thread and 3.5-4 ms on the render thread (`HALO_RT_GPU_LOG` logs them too). The game drew its overlay as fast as its vsynced hidden window allowed (about 145 frames a second, each read back at once) while the viewer showed 60-90 of them, and the rest took GPU time from the ray tracing. The game now paces its frames by the viewer's looks for an overlay (`Bridge::note_overlay_look`, the game's `overlay_pace`; its window unsynchronized): each starts just soon enough (its own frame time and 2.5 ms) to be shared before the viewer's next look. The viewer's GPU time against unpaced (`HALO_RT_NO_PACE=1`), the same build with DLSS: a10's opening 9.5-11.9 to 8.6-11.1 ms, its hologram cutscene 24.7-26.6 to 18.8-20.0 ms (37-42 to 49-53 frames a second), b30 9.6 to 8.8 ms (without DLSS); about one game frame per viewer frame, almost none repeated or left unshown (`HALO_BEVY_PACE_LOG=1` logs the game's side). (The pacing commit's message gives a10 figures from a build without DLSS against one with it: too large.)
       - 23 October: the skinned characters' BLAS refit in place. Each bending part keeps one ray-traced mesh, changed in place (`Tracked::skin_meshes`), registered in Solarik's `SolarikDeformableMeshes`: its BLAS is built with ALLOW_UPDATE, never compacted, and refit when the mesh changes with the same counts (rebuilt every 240 refits); the vendored wgpu-core now issues an update for a PreferUpdate BLAS already built (it only rebuilt). a10, DLSS, paced: BLAS builds 0.4-1.06 to 0.05-0.07 ms a frame, the opening 8.6-11.1 to 7.8-10.2 ms in all. `HALO_RT_NO_REFIT=1` makes a new mesh each time, as before. Still open: the glass pass in a10's hologram cutscene.

## The HUD and menus in the viewer (sub-roadmap)  ← *in progress*

The game still draws its HUD, menus and text into the overlay it shares (at most 2560x1440, copied each frame, blended in the display's encoding). Moving them here draws them at the window's own resolution (an ultrawide's too) with no copy. The game keeps the logic (hud.c, ui_widget.c, its text layout); what it would have drawn is shared as a list, in its order:

1. **Screen quads** (protocol 46): `_rasterizer_psuedo_dynamic_screen_quad_draw` (rasterizer_xbox_dynavobgeom.c), every HUD and menu bitmap. Each quad goes as its four vertices (the window's pixels, its offset applied; each map's texture coordinates already worked out as the screen geometry vertex shader would, from its anchor, scale, offset and texture scale), its maps (bitmap tag and index, wrap, point sampling), the NV2A combiner program the game built for it (stages, constants, final combiner) and its blend (source, destination, operation, constant colour; a meter's alpha kill). The viewer evaluates the combiners as `halo_transparent.wesl` does and blends as the port's overlay does: over a clear texture in the display's encoding, replacing blends adding their coverage, additive ones keeping it, multiplying ones left out; then composites it premultiplied. Quads whose bitmaps aren't a tag's (Bink's frames) stay the game's.
   - [x] Shared and drawn (24 October; the default since protocol 47, `--game-hud` leaves it to the game): b30's HUD (ammo, grenades, crosshair, motion sensor) matches the game's; the menus' bitmaps too, shifted right as the port centers them (`halo_screen_ui_offset`).
   - [x] (Protocol 47) The port's high-res HUD and menu art (port/assets/hud, menus; embedded in halo.exe, chosen by `hud_hires_asset_at` and `menu_art_texture`) in place of the tags' bitmaps, with its sampling (a meter's coverage in green, `point_threshold` segments in red): through a texture channel, the game sharing decoded pixels for any map that isn't a plain tag bitmap (the text atlas too).
   - [x] Draw order: the text comes in the same list (step 2), so the menus' backdrops no longer hide it.
2. **Text**: the high-res text's characters (port/linux/src/text_hires.c: TrueType fonts for the fonts' tags, laid out by the game, rasterized at the window's resolution) as quads of its atlas in the same list, the atlas shared when it changes (protocol 47).
   - [x] Shared and drawn: b30's chapter title as the game's. The old 128-texel text cache (fonts the high-res text doesn't cover) is still the game's.
3. **The motion sensor's blips** (`rasterizer_hud_motion_sensor_blip_draw`) and the **screen effects** (`rasterizer_screen_effect`, the screen flash).
   - [x] (Protocol 48) The motion sensor: its blips, sweep and mask as draws into its own target (the 64-texel sun glow target it borrows, cleared each frame; 256 texels here), colour only and with the sweep's border colour, then the target onto the screen in order. Matches the game's on b30.
   - [x] (Protocol 48) The screen flash: its type, intensity and colour shared, drawn over the HUD as a multiply and an add or subtract (lighten, darken, tint and invert exactly; max and min as lighten and darken). b30's `fade_out` to red matches the game's.
   - [ ] The HUD's screen effects (`rasterizer_screen_effect`: desaturation, light enhancement, convolution) work on the world's picture, which with the viewer drawing the world the game doesn't have: they belong in the viewer's post-processing.
4. **The overlay retired** for play: with the viewer drawing effects, transparent surfaces, decals, fog and the HUD, the game's picture holds only what's left (Bink, the old text cache, the loading bar), and none of it most frames.
   - [x] (Protocol 49) A frame that drew nothing into the overlay shares it empty: no read back (`glReadPixels` of the whole window, a GPU stall) and no upload; the viewer clears its picture once. In play on b30 and a10's opening every frame's overlay is empty (`HALO_RT_OVERLAY_DEBUG=1` shows none). `HALO_BEVY_OVERLAY_ALWAYS=1` reads it back all the same.

## Known risks

- **Solari is experimental.** It needs hardware ray tracing and supports opaque `StandardMaterial` only. Transparent and alpha-tested content needs a fallback.
- **Interior lighting.** Halo's interiors are lit almost entirely by baked radiosity. To look right under Solari, they need emissive light sources recovered from shaders, lens-flare markers and `ligh` tags (Phase 2).
- **32/64-bit split.** The shared-memory bridge has to stay cheap. A frame's state is small (hundreds of objects × nodes), so this should be fine.
- **Legal.** Never commit or redistribute game data or DLSS binaries.
